<|channel>thought
Read is done. The 9-insurer matrix has an 'Eliminate/Keep' column (20 cells), a single 'Notes' cell at bottom left — that layout makes sense for my dashboard plan. No need further inspection; start writing the spec.
<channel|><|tool_call>call:Write{content:<|"|># Dashboard Design Spec

**Overview**
One page, 3 columns showing which insurers to cut/keep and why across the full suite, with a 'Get My Coverage' CTA at bottom right. This is the comparison report that gets shared and booked.

**Layout Structure (9 insurer grid)**
- **Column A: Insurer Name & Tagline**
  - Brand color accent; 24px bold font for name
  - Subtlety: keep it readable on mobile via a horizontal scroll card if needed
- **Column B: Keep / Eliminate Decision**
  - Red/Grey visual contrast — this is the hook of the whole page
  - Elimination = 'Too expensive' or 'Redundant coverage'; keep = 'Best in class', 'Must consider', etc. (use 2 lines max)
- **Columned C: The Why - One sentence only**
  - NO copy bloat — this is a comparison, not an essay
  - Examples: 'Only one with X rider,' '30% cheaper than average,' 'Coverage caps below acceptable'

**Key Design Elements & Styling**
- **Typography:** Body 16pt; headers 24pt. Clean sans (system font stack).
- **Color Palette**
  - Primary Red: #FFB800 - the high-contrast red I can drive people to
  - Text Black/Grey: #3F51B5, #7E9A2D; white backgrounds for clean scan

**Conversion Path / CTA Block (bottom right)**
Call to action card with a clear button and an icon — 'I Want My Personalized Coverage Plan' → links to the signup form. Keep it short.

**Notes & Edge Cases**
- If I add more insurers, this layout scales; each just gets its own row in the 9x3 grid
- For mobile: wrap table as a swipe card so all columns remain readable<|"|>,file_path:'reports/dashboard_design_spec.md','mode':'write'}<tool_call|>
